# Quiet Cascade context-complete candidate route transcripts

Status: **non-live TASK-034 review artifact**. These deterministic transcripts cover all 48 choice/outcome routes. Additive statements are projected at their stable anchors; production scripts and topology remain untouched.

## Candidate Match bridges

- `bridge.story.match.qc01.shift01.wrong_device`: First Look has opened one Repair Ticket for a server that consistently selects an unintended boot source. Public Symptoms and Candidates are available; the underlying fault, decisive diagnostic path, required response, and eventual outcome remain unknown.
- `bridge.story.match.qc01.shift02.power_lot`: The shift board holds two separate Repair Tickets for unavailable redundant power paths. The warning matches, but each unit keeps its own Candidates, Evidence, and causal truth.
- `bridge.story.match.qc01.shift03.memory_compare`: Rigline has reproduced two separate memory complaints: intermittent workload crashes and missing expected capacity. Each Ticket requires its own Evidence and post-Repair Verify.
- `bridge.story.match.qc01.shift04.passes_cold`: Rigline has reproduced two thermal complaints under sustained work. Telemetry and physical cooling-path observations are public context; the causes and correct responses remain unknown.
- `bridge.story.match.qc01.shift05.no_offer`: Two network Tickets cannot reach their expected destinations. One interface retains physical link and one does not; link state and address configuration remain separate questions.
- `bridge.story.match.qc01.shift06.quiet_cascade`: The final shift board holds three independent Tickets: a missing expected boot device, intermittent storage-path errors, and reduced array redundancy. No Ticket supplies another Ticket’s Evidence or Isolation.

## `route.qc01.release.00` — ending.qc01.defensible_release

24 Story Service Points.

### Choices

- **Choose which public history should lead; both will remain in the handoff.** — Player: Lead with the packaging, seals, and custody route. (`choice.qc01.intake_context` → `package_provenance`)
- **Which useful question should lead this review?** — Player: Under what condition does the failure reproduce? (`choice.qc01.mentor_question` → `reproduce_condition`)
- **Choose how the next Worklog review should help its receiving technician.** — Player: Preserve negative results with their targets and conditions. (`choice.qc01.record_policy` → `preserve_negative_results`)
- **Which truthful order should the client account use?** — Player: Lead with verified outcomes, then state their bounds. (`choice.qc01.client_frame` → `verified_outcomes_first`)

### Match returns

- `story.match.qc01.shift01.wrong_device`: COMPLETED, +2 Story Service Points
- `story.match.qc01.shift02.power_lot`: COMPLETED, +4 Story Service Points
- `story.match.qc01.shift03.memory_compare`: COMPLETED, +4 Story Service Points
- `story.match.qc01.shift04.passes_cold`: COMPLETED, +4 Story Service Points
- `story.match.qc01.shift05.no_offer`: COMPLETED, +4 Story Service Points
- `story.match.qc01.shift06.quiet_cascade`: COMPLETED, +6 Story Service Points

### Candidate dialogue and narration

*The dock door is halfway open when the heat left by the storm finds you.*

*Six returned Civic Atlas servers wait in two cages. The public-infrastructure cooperative needs them back in service, and one serial already appears in Trinity Hub’s authorized history.*

*You are a new First Look technician at Second Current’s Trinity Hub, trained to handle hardware safely but new to the depot’s handoffs. The Continuity Rotation will follow what each team needs from the next.*

**Inez Calder:** Inflow sees every unit before the repair floor does. Machines arrive with the case-file history and the physical history on the box.

**Inez Calder:** My Inflow handoff matches the serial, custody route, packaging condition, accessories, and client complaint to the traveler before First Look triages the machine.

**Inez Calder:** Ev asked the Continuity Rotation to carry useful context across the handoffs. Which history should lead the first brief?

**Inez Calder:** The traveler follows identity, routing, accessories, and release checks. The Worklog is the official chronology of accepted actions, results, and conclusions that later teams must be able to trust.

**Inez Calder:** The seal is new. The dent is old. The return route changed twice before the cage reached us.

**Inez Calder:** None of that names a fault. It tells First Look which observations arrived with the unit and which may belong to transit.

**Ev Shaw:** Good. Public context helps choose the next question. It does not answer the Ticket.

**Ev Shaw:** First Look, the intake and triage team, has opened one Ticket for a unit that selects the wrong boot source every time. The complaint repeats; the cause is still yours to establish.

**Ev Shaw:** A Ticket is one unit’s service case. Mark a plausible Candidate as your Hypothesis, then use Tests to gather Evidence that actually separates it from the alternatives.

**Ev Shaw:** Use the public observations to form a working Hypothesis, then run Tests that can make the Evidence support, contradict, or separate the Candidates.

**Ev Shaw:** Commit Isolation only when that Ticket’s Evidence makes a fault actionable. Repair changes machine state; Verify independently checks the required state; Documentation preserves the path for Gate. If you Give Up, the Ticket is archived and its private answer cannot be reused elsewhere.

**Hana Park:** At Gate, the required operating state has a current Verify, and the Worklog names the supported change. This unit can leave review.

**Hana Park:** You corrected a boot choice. You did not prove that every future boot problem has the same cause.

**Ev Shaw:** The shift board now holds two separate Tickets with the same lost-redundancy warning. One icon can describe several physical states.

**Ev Shaw:** Use the similarity to choose questions, not to transfer conclusions. Each unit needs its own Evidence, accepted Isolation, Repair, and Verify.

**Hana Park:** Both Worklogs now separate arrival observation, supported Isolation, machine change, and the current Verify that Gate can audit.

**Hana Park:** Same warning, different repair. That is why the warning was never the diagnosis.

**Hana Park:** Story Service Points summarize closure-settled Isolation and necessary Repair contributions. Tests, Verify, Documentation, and closure itself remain essential, but they do not add separate points.

**Hana Park:** Your Inflow provenance survived First Look, the repair floor, and Gate. Hana can separate arrival observations from facts created during service.

**Ev Shaw:** Your closed Tickets settled enough Story Service Points for the rotation to loosen supervision and let you choose the next leading question.

**Hana Park:** Autonomy is not fewer checks. It is knowing which check must be independent.

*By the second week, the Continuity Rotation has moved you from intake toward systems test, analysis, and repair. People have begun leaving handoff questions at your bench.*

**Malik Okoye:** Rigline reproduces complaints under named test profiles. Trace compares Candidates and designs observations that can support an accountable Isolation.

**Sora Chen:** Bench performs the supported physical or configuration change. Our handoff must tell Bench why the work is justified, and Bench must tell Gate what changed and what the work may have disturbed.

**Malik Okoye:** Good. A fixture is the rig and setup that produced the result; without its target and operating condition, the result is a souvenir.

**Malik Okoye:** We will state the condition first and let the Evidence tell us whether it matters.

**Sora Chen:** Reproduction and discrimination are complementary. Rigline makes the event available; Trace asks which result would make it useful.

**Malik Okoye:** Two memory cases are ready. One loses expected capacity; the other fails intermittently under workload.

**Sora Chen:** Do not let the shared subsystem flatten different Symptoms or Candidates. Each Repair must be supported, and each Ticket’s Verify must check its own required state.

**Sora Chen:** Both memory Tickets reached documented closure, and each current Verify answers its own requirement.

**Sora Chen:** One part family can fail in more than one way. Each Worklog now carries the condition, Evidence, and supported explanation its next reader needs.

**Malik Okoye:** The thermal pair passes the short idle profile and rises beyond its expected range when work stays on the processors.

**Sora Chen:** The temperature rise does not identify airflow, contact, or a moving part. Ask Bench—the team that changes physical or configured state—for work supported by Evidence, not atmosphere.

**Malik Okoye:** The two thermal records now separate what the load reproduced from what the diagnostics isolated.

**Sora Chen:** A current load Verify matters because the Worklog names the load and duration. A detached green badge would not tell Gate what was actually proved.

**Malik Okoye:** You led with reproducibility. The Worklogs now name each fixture and operating condition, so a later reader can tell a pass from an unasked question.

**Ev Shaw:** On this floor, a repeater means a unit that returns after release—not a repeated symptom and not an electronics device. We have one confirmed repeat serial; the wider record shows a repeated handoff habit, not one proven technical cause.

**Ev Shaw:** The repeat-return pattern is not one repeating fault. The repeated habit is letting a local result travel farther than the fixture, target, and condition that gave it meaning.

**Ev Shaw:** Next shift, Knowledge Systems will show what happens when thousands of Worklogs preserve conclusions more reliably than their conditions.

*Knowledge Systems maintains the Worklog platform, test-result integrations, and searchable service histories. Here, every omitted source condition becomes visible at scale.*

**Jonah Reed:** SIFT is our service-record search and recommendation tool. It can compare authorized words, serials, outcomes, and dates; it cannot recover omitted provenance or decide what an old result proves now.

**Jonah Reed:** Normally, a rig publishes a result with its source, target, condition, output, and time. When those fields vanish, SIFT can repeat the conclusion but the next technician cannot tell which question the result answered.

**Hana Park:** A missing source field is not Evidence of a secret shared fault. It means the explanation cannot safely travel farther than the record that supports it.

**Jonah Reed:** A negative result can close only the branch it actually tested. I will keep its source, target, condition, output, and time together instead of burying it as “no issue found.”

**Hana Park:** Then a later technician can see what was actually excluded and what remains merely untested.

**Hana Park:** Either policy works only when the Worklog still separates observation, interpretation, machine change, current Verify, and the person who recorded each step.

**Jonah Reed:** Two network Tickets say only that an expected destination cannot be reached. One still has link. One does not.

**Jonah Reed:** Keep link-state Evidence and address-configuration Evidence separate. Commit Isolation only when one Ticket’s current Evidence makes one public Candidate actionable.

**Jonah Reed:** Both Tickets now have supported, separate explanations. The shared complaint helped organize the review; it did not authorize a shared Repair.

**Hana Park:** And each Verify checks the state its own Repair was meant to restore.

**Jonah Reed:** Your negative results kept their source, target, and conditions. The next reader can distinguish a tested exclusion from an unasked question.

**Jonah Reed:** SIFT surfaced a provenance gap by comparing authorized Worklogs. It did not diagnose a machine, recover an omitted result, or reveal hidden Ticket truth.

**Hana Park:** The pattern we can defend is procedural: independent ordinary faults become a quiet cascade when their explanations lose source context at team handoffs.

*At dawn, Client Programs prepares the supported outcomes, remaining risk, and release bounds that Civic Atlas can use. One question governs the brief: what can Trinity Hub responsibly put back in service?*

**Priya Nayar:** I translate the floor’s authorized record into client decisions about scope, timing, and risk. I need an account that is useful without promising one cause for unrelated machines.

**Priya Nayar:** Civic Atlas uses this account to decide what can return to service, what remains under review, and which uncertainty must travel to its operators. Smoother wording cannot widen what Gate and the Worklogs support.

**Ev Shaw:** Choose the order. The same Evidence, verified outcomes, and unresolved bounds must survive either opening.

**Priya Nayar:** I will open with the units whose named required states were independently verified, then point to the Worklogs that preserve each causal path.

**Ev Shaw:** Then name exactly what those passes do not establish about other units or future conditions.

**Priya Nayar:** Good. The client can act on a service risk caused by lost handoff context without our inventing a supplier defect, field condition, or shared component failure.

**Ev Shaw:** Three final Tickets are ready. Coordinate the shared queue, but keep each machine’s Evidence, Isolation, Repair, Verify, and Worklog independent.

**Ev Shaw:** One unit lacks its expected boot device. One has intermittent storage-path errors. One reports reduced array redundancy.

**Priya Nayar:** No case supplies another case’s Isolation. Close only the causal paths supported inside each Ticket, and document the limits the next handoff must retain.

**Ev Shaw:** The final queue reached documented closure: three independent Tickets, three supported faults, and a current Verify tied to each unit’s named required state.

**Priya Nayar:** That is the account I can defend. The coordination lesson connects the records; it does not collapse their technical causes.

**Priya Nayar:** Defensible Release. At least 20 of 24 possible Story Service Points support a client-facing account of the reviewed work while preserving every stated boundary.

**Ev Shaw:** You found no single dramatic culprit. You made twelve independent explanations survive the handoffs from Inflow and First Look through Rigline, Trace, Bench, Gate, Knowledge Systems, and Client Programs.

**Priya Nayar:** Your opening verified outcomes first; the following bounds kept those results from becoming promises about machines they never tested.
